\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{n},\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\kappa_{2}\varepsilon\left\langle\partial_{t}(|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{1}\varepsilon\left\langle(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n})\boldsymbol{e},\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-3\lambda_{2}\varepsilon\left\langle(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{n})^{2}\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(3.36)

\displaystyle=J_{1}+J_{2}+\cdots+J_{10}.

We will estimate each term on the last line. For the first three terms, by Young’s inequality (noting (
2.47
)), we have

\displaystyle\left|{J_{1}}\right|+\left|{J_{2}}\right|+\left|{J_{3}}\right|\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{9}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the term
J_{5}
, similarly we have

\displaystyle\left|{J_{5}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{9}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the terms
J_{4}
and
J_{6}
, by Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities,

\displaystyle\left|{J_{4}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{9}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{6}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{2}\varepsilon\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{9}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the terms
J_{7}
and
J_{8}
, by similar argument we have

\displaystyle\left|{J_{7}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{9}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{8}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{9}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the terms
J_{9}
and
J_{10}
, we apply (
2.30
) and (
2.41
) respectively to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{J_{9}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\nu_{\infty}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|\,|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{9}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{10}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}\right)+\frac{1}{9}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Altogether, from (
3
) we infer

\displaystyle\varepsilon\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}

(3.37)

\displaystyle\quad+C\nu_{\infty}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|\,|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}\right).

Integrating this with respect to
t
and using (
3.1
), (
3.21
), and (
3.22
), we obtain

(3.38)

\varepsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C\varepsilon^{-3}(1+t^{3})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2},

which gives (
3.32
), but not exactly (
3.31
) since this bound still depends on
t
. Inequality (
3.31
) will be derived after the rest of the proposition is shown.

Next, note that for
t\geq t_{1}
(as defined in Proposition
3.3
), the estimates (
3.3
), (
3.23
), and (
3
) imply

\displaystyle\varepsilon\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{-1}(1+\rho_{1}^{2})\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\rho_{0}\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\nu_{\infty}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|\,|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}.

The uniform Gronwall inequality (noting (
3.3
) and (
3.23
)) yields

(3.39)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{-4},\quad\forall t\geq t_{1}+1,

where
C
is a constant independent of
t
,
\varepsilon
, and
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
.
Noting (
3.39
), integrating (
3
) over
(t,t+1)
and rearranging the terms, we obtain (
3.33
).
Similarly, integrating over
(t,t+\delta)
instead, by the uniform Gronwall inequality and (
3.24
), we have (
3.34
).

Finally, applying (
3.34
) with
\delta=1
, we have

\varepsilon^{4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq\mu_{2},\quad\forall t\geq 1.

where
\mu_{2}
is independent of
\varepsilon
and
t
.
This, together with the bound (
3.38
) for
t\leq 1
, implies (
3.31
).
Thus, the proof is completed.
∎

Proposition 3.5
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\geq 0
,

(3.40)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2},

(3.41)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{4}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-4}(1+t^{3})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2},

where
C
is a constant which is independent of
\varepsilon
,
t
, and
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
.

Moreover, there exists
t_{3}
depending on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
such that for all
t\geq t_{3}
,

(3.42)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{t}^{t+1}\varepsilon^{5}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t}^{t+1}\varepsilon^{5}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{4}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\rho_{3},

where
\rho_{3}
is independent of
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
,
\varepsilon
, and
t
.

Finally, let
\delta>0
be arbitrary. Then for all
t\geq\delta
,

(3.43)

\varepsilon^{4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\varepsilon^{5}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\varepsilon^{5}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{4}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\mu_{3},

where
\mu_{3}
depends on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
, but is independent of
\varepsilon
and
t
.

Proof.

Taking the inner product of the second equation in (
2.49
) with
\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{n},\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\kappa_{1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\kappa_{2}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n},\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{1}\left\langle(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{n})\boldsymbol{e},\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\lambda_{2}\left\langle(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{n})^{3}\boldsymbol{e},\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

Therefore, after rearranging the terms, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\langle\boldsymbol{H}_{n},\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n},\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda_{1}\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\lambda_{2}\left\langle(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{n})^{3}\boldsymbol{e},\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{6}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

(3.44)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where we used Young’s inequality and the Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{6}
. Rearranging the terms in this inequality, noting (
3.21
) and (
3.31
), we then have

(3.45)

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{-4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2},

which, together with (
3.2
), implies (
3.40
).

Similarly, taking the inner product of the first equation in (
2.49
) with
\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}
, rearranging the terms, and applying Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\sigma\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n},\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\sigma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{n},\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(3.46)

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}+\cdots+I_{7}.

For the terms
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
, and
I_{3}
, we apply Young’s inequality and (
2.47
) to infer

\displaystyle\left|{I_{1}}\right|+\left|{I_{2}}\right|+\left|{I_{3}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-1}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma}{8}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the terms
I_{4}
and
I_{5}
, by Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities (noting (
2.47
)), we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{4}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon^{-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{I_{5}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon^{-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}+\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the last two terms, we use (
2.30
) and (
2.41
) respectively, to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{6}}\right|+\left|{I_{7}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\nu_{\infty}\varepsilon^{-1}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|\,|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+C\varepsilon^{-1}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}\right)+\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Altogether, substituting these estimates into (
3
) and integrating both sides with respect to
t
(noting (
3.32
), (
3.45
), and (
3.21
)), we infer

(3.47)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C\varepsilon^{-5}(1+t^{3})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}.

Next, applying the operator
\Delta
to the second equation in (
2.49
) and taking the inner product with
\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}
, we obtain by similar argument as in (
3
),

(3.48)

\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C\varepsilon^{-5}(1+t^{3})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}.

This, together with (
3.45
) and (
3.47
), implies (
3.41
).

Moreover, rearranging the terms in (
3
), then applying (
3.23
) and (
3.33
) give

(3.49)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C(1+\rho_{1}^{3}+\rho_{2}),\quad\forall t\geq t_{1}+1.

Integrating both sides of (
3
) over
(t,t+1)
, noting (
3.23
), (
3.33
), and (
3.33
), we obtain

(3.50)

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{t+1}\varepsilon^{5}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C(\rho_{0}+\rho_{1}^{2}+\rho_{2}),\quad\forall t\geq t_{1}+1.

Similarly, corresponding to (
3.48
), we have

(3.51)

\int_{t}^{t+1}\varepsilon^{5}\left\|{\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C,\quad\forall t\geq t_{1}+1,

where
C
depends only on
\rho_{0}
,
\rho_{1}
,
\rho_{2}
, and
|\mathscr{O}|
.
Altogether, we infer the inequality (
3.42
) for all
t\geq t_{1}+1
from (
3.49
), (
3.50
), and (
3.51
).

Finally, noting (
3.24
) and (
3.34
), we repeat the argument leading to (
3.49
), (
3.50
), and (
3.51
), but integrating over
(t,t+\delta)
instead. This yields (
3.43
), thus completing the proof of the proposition.
∎

Proposition 3.6
.

For all
t>0
,

(3.52)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\varepsilon^{-4}(1+t^{3}+t^{-1})

where
M_{1}
depends on
|\mathscr{O}|
,
\nu_{\infty}
, and
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}
(but is independent of
\varepsilon
).

Proof.

From inequalities (
3
), (
3.2
), and (
3.21
), we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\nu_{\infty}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|\,|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{-1}\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+CM\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}

(3.53)

\displaystyle\quad+C\nu_{\infty}\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|\,|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+C\left(M+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}\right).

Now, by using (
3.22
) for
t\leq 1
and (
3.24
) for
t\geq 1
, we have

(3.54)

\int_{t}^{t+1}\varepsilon^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{1},\quad\forall t\geq 0,

where
C_{1}
depends only on the coefficients of (
1.1
),
|\mathscr{O}|
,
\nu_{\infty}
, and
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}
. Moreover, by (
3.4
) for
\delta=1
, we have

(3.55)

\int_{t}^{t+1}\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{4}+\varepsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)||\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}s\leq C_{0},\quad\forall t\geq 0,

where
C_{0}
depends only on the coefficients of (
1.1
),
|\mathscr{O}|
,
\nu_{\infty}
, and
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
. Furthermore, by (
3.1
) and (
3.22
),

(3.56)

\int_{0}^{t}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)||\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}s\leq C(1+t^{3})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2},\quad\forall t\geq 0.

Altogether, inequalities (
3.54
), (
3.55
), and (
3.56
) imply the required inequality for the first term by Corollary
A.2
.

Finally, using the second equation in (
2.49
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+(\kappa_{1}+\lambda_{1})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+(\kappa_{2}+\lambda_{2})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{6}

\displaystyle\leq M_{1}\varepsilon^{-4}(1+t^{3}+t^{-1})+CM+C\varepsilon^{-3}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{6},

where in the last step we used (
3.2
), the Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{6}
, and (
3.21
). Thus, the proof is now complete.
∎

Proposition 3.7
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\geq 0
,

(3.57)

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-8}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2},

(3.58)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-7}(1+t^{3})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2},

where
C
is a constant which is independent of
\varepsilon
,
t
, and
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
.

Moreover, there exists
t_{3}
depending on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
such that for all
t\geq t_{3}
,

(3.59)

\varepsilon^{8}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{t}^{t+1}\varepsilon^{7}\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\rho_{3},

where
\rho_{3}
is independent of
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
,
\varepsilon
, and
t
.

Finally, let
\delta>0
be arbitrary. Then for all
t\geq\delta
,

(3.60)

\varepsilon^{8}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\varepsilon^{7}\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\mu_{3},

where
\mu_{3}
depends on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
, but is independent of
\varepsilon
and
t
.

Proof.

Taking the inner product of the first equation in (
2.49
) with
-\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}
gives

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n},\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\sigma\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{n}\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n},\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\left\langle\nabla\Delta\Pi_{n}\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\gamma\left\langle\nabla(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{n}),\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(3.61)

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\nabla(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n})),\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Differentiating the second equation in (
2.49
) with respect to
t
, then taking the inner product of the resulting equation with
-\varepsilon\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n},\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{1}\varepsilon\left\langle\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n},\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\varepsilon\left\langle\partial_{t}(|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(3.62)

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda_{1}\varepsilon\left\langle(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n})\boldsymbol{e},\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-3\lambda_{2}\varepsilon\left\langle(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{n})^{2}\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Adding (
3
) and (
3
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=(\sigma+\kappa_{1}\varepsilon)\left\langle\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n},\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\sigma\left\langle\nabla\Pi_{n}\Phi_{d}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\langle\nabla\Delta\Pi_{n}\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\nabla(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{n}),\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\gamma\left\langle\nabla(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n})),\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\varepsilon\left\langle\partial_{t}(|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda_{1}\varepsilon\left\langle(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n})\boldsymbol{e},\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-3\lambda_{2}\varepsilon\left\langle(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{n})^{2}\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(3.63)

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}+\cdots+I_{10}.

Each term on the last line can be estimated analogously to (
3
). For the first three terms, by Young’s inequality,

\left|{I_{1}}\right|+\left|{I_{2}}\right|+\left|{I_{3}}\right|\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the term
I_{4}
and
I_{5}
, by Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, and the Sobolev embedding we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{4}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\left\|{\nabla(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{n})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{I_{5}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\left\|{\nabla(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}_{n}))}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the terms
I_{6}
,
I_{7}
, and
I_{8}
, similarly we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{6}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 3\kappa_{2}\varepsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{I_{7}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{1}\varepsilon\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{I_{8}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 3\lambda_{2}\varepsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}.

For the last two terms in (
3
), we apply Young’s inequality, (
2.33
), and (
2.42
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{9}}\right|+\left|{I_{10}}\right|\leq C\nu_{\infty}\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}+\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{4}\right)+C\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}||\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{8}\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Altogether, the above estimates for
I_{j}
, where
j=1,2,\ldots,9
, imply

\displaystyle\varepsilon\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}

(3.64)

\displaystyle\quad+C\nu_{\infty}\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}+\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{4}\right)+C\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}||\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right).

Integrating this over
(0,t)
, noting (
3.1
), (
3.40
), and (
3.41
), we obtain

\varepsilon\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C\varepsilon^{-7}(1+t^{3})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2},

which implies (
3.58
), but not (
3.57
) due to the dependence on
t
. The proof of (
3.57
) will be given after we show (
3.59
) and (
3.60
).

Next, by using (
3.42
) and applying the uniform Gronwall inequality, we obtain

\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{-8},\quad\forall t\geq t_{3},

where
C
is independent of
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
and
t
. Integrating (
3
) over
(t,t+1)
and using the above inequality then yields (
3.59
).
By similar argument using the uniform Gronwall inequality, but applying (
3.43
) and integrating over
(t,t+\delta)
instead, we obtain (
3.60
).

Finally, it remains to show (
3.57
). By (
3.60
) for
\delta=1
, we obtain a bound on
\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
for
t\geq 1
which is independent of
t
. This, together with (
3.41
) for
t\leq 1
, yields (
3.57
).
This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Proposition 3.8
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\geq 0
,

(3.65)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-8}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2},

(3.66)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{5}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{-9}(1+t^{3})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2},

where
C
is a constant which is independent of
\varepsilon
,
t
, and
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
.

Moreover, there exists
t_{4}
depending on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
such that for all
t\geq t_{4}
,

(3.67)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{8}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}+\int_{t}^{t+1}\varepsilon^{9}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t}^{t+1}\varepsilon^{9}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{5}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\rho_{4},

where
\rho_{4}
is independent of
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
,
\varepsilon
, and
t
.

Finally, let
\delta>0
be arbitrary. Then for all
t\geq\delta
,

(3.68)

\varepsilon^{8}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\varepsilon^{9}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\varepsilon^{9}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{5}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\mu_{4},

where
\mu_{4}
depends on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
, but is independent of
\varepsilon
and
t
.

Proof.

The proof of this proposition follows by similar argument as in Proposition
3.5
. Firstly, taking the inner product of the second equation in (
2.49
) with
\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}
and integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{1}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\Big|\left\langle\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n},\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big|+\kappa_{2}\,\Big|\left\langle\nabla(|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda_{1}\,\Big|\left\langle\boldsymbol{e}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}),\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big|+3\lambda_{2}\,\Big|\left\langle(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{n})^{2}(\boldsymbol{e}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n})\boldsymbol{e},\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{1}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\lambda_{1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+(\kappa_{2}+3\lambda_{2})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

by the same argument as in (
3
). By (
3.40
) and (
3.57
), we then have (
3.65
).

Next, taking the inner product of the first equation in (
2.49
) with
\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{H}_{n}
and continuing along the same line as in (
3
), (
3.47
), and (
3.50
), we obtain (
3.66
).

Finally, the proof of (
3.67
) and (
3.68
) follows the same argument as that of (
3.42
) and (
3.43
), respectively. Further details are omitted for brevity.
∎

Proposition 3.9
.

For all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq M_{2}\varepsilon^{-8}(1+t^{3}+t^{-1}),

where
M_{2}
depends on
|\mathscr{O}|
,
\nu_{\infty}
, and
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}
(but is independent of
\varepsilon
).

Proof.

From (
3
), noting (
3.40
), we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\nu_{\infty}\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}+C\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}||\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\varepsilon^{-4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\left(1+\varepsilon^{-2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\nu_{\infty}\left(1+\varepsilon^{-4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}+C\left(\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}||\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right).

Now, note that using (
3.4
) and (
3.43
) with
\delta=1
, and (
3.22
) for
t\leq 1
, we have

\int_{t}^{t+1}\varepsilon^{5}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t}^{t+1}\varepsilon^{3}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t}^{t+1}\varepsilon\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)||\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C,\quad\forall t\geq 0.

Furthermore, by (
3.41
), for all
t\geq 0
,

\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{5}\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{3}\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C(1+t^{3}),

where
C
depends on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}
.
The required result then follows from Corollary
A.2
.
∎

The bounds proved so far are summarised in the following proposition for ease of reference later. We do not track the dependence of the constants on
\varepsilon
here.

Proposition 3.10
.

Let
k=0,1,2
, or
3
.

(1)

For all
t\geq 0
,

(3.69)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k}}^{2},

(3.70)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k+2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(1+t^{3})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k}}^{2},

where
C
is a constant depending only on the coefficients of (
1.1
).

(2)

There exists
t_{k}
depending on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
such that, for all
t\geq t_{k}
,

(3.71)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k}}^{2}+\int_{t}^{t+1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k+2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t}^{t+1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\alpha_{k},

where
\alpha_{k}
is a constant independent of
t
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
.

(3)

For all
t\geq\delta
, where
\delta>0
is arbitrary,

(3.72)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k}}^{2}+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k+2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\beta_{k},

where
\beta_{k}
is a constant independent of
t
(but may depend on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
).

(4)

For all
t>0
,

(3.73)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k}}^{2}\leq M_{s}(1+t^{3}+t^{-1}).

Here,
M_{s}
is a constant depending on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}}
, where
s:=\max\{0,k-1\}
, but is independent of
t
.

Suppose now that
k=2
or
3
.

(1)

For all
t\geq 0
,

(3.74)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k-2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(1+t^{3})\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k}}^{2},

where
C
is a constant depending only on the coefficients of (
1.1
).

(2)

There exists
t_{k}
depending on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
such that, for all
t\geq t_{k}
,

(3.75)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k-2}}^{2}+\int_{t}^{t+1}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k-2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\alpha_{k},

where
\alpha_{k}
is a constant independent of
t
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
.

(3)

For all
t\geq\delta
, where
\delta>0
is arbitrary,

(3.76)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k-2}}^{2}+\int_{t}^{t+\delta}\left\|{\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{u}_{n}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k-2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\beta_{k},

where
\beta_{k}
is a constant independent of
t
(but may depend on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}
).

(4)

For all
t\geq 0
,

(3.77)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{n}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k-2}}^{2}\leq M_{k}(1+t^{3}+t^{-1}),

where
M_{k}
is a constant depending on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k}}
, but is independent of
t
.

The following result on existence and uniqueness of solution to (
1.1
) is immediate.

Theorem 3.11
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}
be a given initial data. There exists a unique global weak solution
\boldsymbol{u}
to (
1.1
) in the sense of Definition
2.1
. This solution satisfies

(3.78)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq M_{1}(1+t^{3}+t^{-1}).

If
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{2}
, then this solution is a strong solution satisfying

(3.79)

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\leq M_{2}(1+t^{3}+t^{-1}),

where the constants
M_{k}
, where
k=1,2
, depend only on the coefficients of (
1.1
),
|\mathscr{O}|
,
\nu_{\infty}
, and
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{k}}
.

Furthermore, if
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{3}
, then the solution
\boldsymbol{u}
belongs to
C([0,T];\mathbb{H}^{3})\cap L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{H}^{5})
.

Proof.

This follows from a standard compactness argument, making use of the uniform estimates summarised in Proposition
3.10
and the Aubin–Lions lemma (cf.
[
48
, Theorem 2.2]
). The inequalities (
3.78
) and (
3.79
) follow from (
3.73
) and (
3.77
), respectively.
∎

4.
Long-time behaviour of the solution

4.1.
Auxiliary results on dynamical systems

First, we recall some basic facts and terminologies in the theory of dynamical systems
[
40
,
43
]
. A well-posed system of time-dependent PDEs on a Banach space
(X,\left\|{\cdot}\right\|_{X})
generates a strongly continuous (nonlinear) semigroup

\displaystyle S(t):X\to X,\quad S(t)u_{0}=u(t)\quad\text{for }t\geq 0.

Therefore,
\big(X,\{S(t)\}_{t\geq 0}\big)
is a semi-dynamical system.

Definition 4.1

(Global attractor)
.

A subset
\mathcal{A}\subset X
is a (compact)
global attractor
for
S(t)
if

(1)

it is compact in
X
,

(2)

it is invariant, i.e.
S(t)\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}
,
\forall t\geq 0
,

(3)

for any bounded set
B\subset X
,

\lim_{t\to+\infty}\text{dist}\big(S(t)B,\mathcal{A}\big)=0,

where dist denotes the Hausdorff semi-metric between sets defined by

\text{dist}(A,B):=\sup_{a\in A}\inf_{b\in B}\left\|{a-b}\right\|_{X}.

Note that the global attractor, if it exists, is unique. Next, a bounded set
B_{0}\subset X
is a bounded absorbing set for
S(t)
if, for any bounded set
B\subset X
, there exists
t_{0}:=t_{0}(B)
such that
S(t)B\subset B_{0}
for all
t\geq t_{0}
. The semigroup
S(t)
is said to be dissipative in
X
if it possesses a bounded absorbing set
B_{0}\subset X
. Moreover, the
\omega
-limit set of a set
B
is defined as

\omega(B):=\left\{y\in X:\exists t_{n}\to+\infty\text{ and }x_{n}\in B\text{ such that }S(t_{n})x_{n}\to y\right\}=\overline{\bigcap_{t\geq 0}\bigcup_{s\geq t}S(s)B}.

If
B=\{v\}
is a singleton, then we write
\omega(v)
in lieu of
\omega(\{v\})
.
For any
u_{0}\in X
, it can be seen that
\text{d}\big(u(t),\omega(u_{0})\big)\to 0
as
t\to+\infty
, where
\text{d}(u(t),B):=\inf_{\varphi\in B}\left\|{u(t)-\varphi}\right\|_{X}
.
The following abstract theorem shows a relation between the existence of a compact absorbing set and the global attractor.

Theorem 4.2
.

If
S(t)
is a dissipative semigroup on
X
which has a compact absorbing set
K
, then there exists a connected global attractor
\mathcal{A}=\omega(K)
.

If the semigroup admits a global Lyapunov function, then more regular structures on the global attractor can be deduced. We mention the following results from
[
40
]
.

Definition 4.3

(Global Lyapunov function)
.

Let
E\subset X
and
\mathcal{L}:E\to\mathbb{R}
be a continuous function. The function
\mathcal{L}
is a
global Lyapunov function
for
S(t)
on
E
if

(1)

for all
u_{0}\in E
, the function
t\mapsto\mathcal{L}(S(t)u_{0})
is non-increasing,

(2)

if
\mathcal{L}(S(t)u_{0})=\mathcal{L}(u_{0})
for some
t>0
, then
u_{0}
is a fixed point of
S(t)
.

In particular, the second condition above implies that the system can have no periodic orbits.
Next, denote the set of fixed points of
S(t)
by
\mathcal{N}
, and define the unstable set of
B
to be the set

(4.1)

\mathcal{M}^{\rm{un}}(B):=\left\{u_{0}\in B:S(t)u_{0}\text{ is defined for all $t\in\mathbb{R}$ and}\lim_{t\to-\infty}\text{d}(u(t),B)=0\right\}.

Note that if the semigroup
S(t)
is injective, then
S(t)u_{0}
is defined for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, i.e.
S(t)
defines a dynamical system. In this case, the first condition in (
4.1
) is redundant.
The following result shows that if the semigroup possesses a global Lyapunov function, then the only possible limit set of individual trajectories are the fixed points.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
S(t)
be a semigroup with global attractor
\mathcal{A}
, which admits a global Lyapunov function on
E
. Then

(1)

\omega(u_{0})\subset\mathcal{N}
for every
u_{0}\in X
(i.e.
\text{d}(u(t),\mathcal{N})\to 0
as
t\to+\infty
),

(2)

\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{M}^{\rm{un}}(\mathcal{N})
.

We also need the following notion of the fractal dimension of a set.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
X
be a compact subset of
E
. For
\epsilon>0
, let
N_{\epsilon}(X)
be the minimal number of balls of radius
\epsilon
which are necessary to cover
X
. The
fractal dimension
of
X
is the quantity

\text{dim}_{\mathrm{F}}X:=\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\frac{\log_{2}N_{\epsilon}(X)}{\log_{2}(1/\epsilon)}.

Note that
\text{dim}_{\mathrm{F}}X\in[0,\infty]
. The quantity
\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}(X):=\log_{2}N_{\epsilon}(X)
is called the Kolmogorov
\epsilon
-entropy of
X
.

To show that the global attractor has a finite fractal dimension, we follow a general method based on the smoothing (or squeezing) property of the semigroup proposed in
[
20
,
21
,
56
,
57
]
, whose ideas can be traced back to Ladyzhenskaya
[
31
]
.

Theorem 4.6
.

Let
X
be a compact subset of a Banach space
E_{1}
. Suppose that
E_{1}\hookrightarrow E
is a compact embedding. Let
L:X\to X
be a map such that
L(X)=X
and

(4.2)

\left\|{Lx_{1}-Lx_{2}}\right\|_{E_{1}}\leq\alpha\left\|{x_{1}-x_{2}}\right\|_{E},\quad\forall x_{1},x_{2}\in X.

Then the fractal dimension of
X
is finite and satisfies

\text{dim}_{\mathrm{F}}X\leq\mathcal{H}_{1/4\alpha}\big(B_{E_{1}}\big),

where
\alpha
is the constant in (
4.2
),
\mathcal{H}_{1/4\alpha}
is the Kolmogorov
1/4\alpha
-entropy as defined in Definition
4.5
, and
B_{E_{1}}
is the unit ball in
E_{1}
(which is relatively compact in
E
).

The above theorem will be applied in our case with
X=\mathcal{A}
, where
\mathcal{A}
is the global attractor, and
L:=S(t_{0})
for some
t_{0}>0
.

4.2.
Uniform estimates on difference of two solutions

To infer the existence of an exponential attractor and estimate the dimension of the global attractor, we need to establish continuous dependence and smoothing estimates for the difference of two solutions in various norms. Without loss of generality, we will assume that
\sigma-\varepsilon\kappa_{1}>0
to simplify presentation (otherwise, the interpolation inequality (
2.16
) could be used to complete the proof in the general case).

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}(t)
and
\boldsymbol{v}(t)
be solutions of (
1.1
) corresponding to initial data
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}
and
\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}
, respectively. Then for any
t>0
,

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}(t)-\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}(s)-\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(s)-\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq Ce^{Ct}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}-\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where
C
depends only on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}
,
\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}
, and the coefficients of the equation (
1.1
).

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{H}_{1}
and
\boldsymbol{H}_{2}
be the effective field corresponding to
\boldsymbol{u}
and
\boldsymbol{v}
, respectively. Let
\boldsymbol{w}:=\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{v}
and
\boldsymbol{B}:=\boldsymbol{H}_{1}-\boldsymbol{H}_{2}
. Then, noting (
2.46
),
\boldsymbol{w}
and
\boldsymbol{B}
satisfy

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{w}

\displaystyle=\sigma\boldsymbol{B}+\sigma\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w})-\varepsilon\Delta\boldsymbol{B}-\varepsilon\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w})-\gamma(\boldsymbol{w}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{1}+\boldsymbol{v}\times\boldsymbol{B})

(4.3)

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\big(\boldsymbol{w}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u})+\boldsymbol{v}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w})\big)+\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{u})-\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{v})+\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{u})-\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{v}),

(4.4)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{B}

\displaystyle=\Delta\boldsymbol{w}+\kappa_{1}\boldsymbol{w}-\kappa_{2}\left(|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{w}+((\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{v}\right)+\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v}),

with initial data
\boldsymbol{w}(0)=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}-\boldsymbol{v}_{0}
.

Taking the inner product of (
4.2
) with
\boldsymbol{w}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma\left\langle\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\sigma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.5)

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\Phi_{d}(\boldsymbol{w}),\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{u})-\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{u})-\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Taking the inner product of (
4.4
) with
\sigma\boldsymbol{w}
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma\left\langle\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\sigma\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\sigma\kappa_{1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\sigma\kappa_{2}\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}||\boldsymbol{w}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\sigma\kappa_{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\sigma\kappa_{2}\left\langle(\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.6)

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Furthermore, taking the inner product of (
4.4
) with
-\varepsilon\Delta\boldsymbol{w}
we obtain

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\kappa_{1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\kappa_{2}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{w},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\varepsilon\kappa_{2}\left\langle((\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{v},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.7)

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Similarly, using (
4.4
) again and noting that
\boldsymbol{v}\times\boldsymbol{w}=\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{w}
,

(4.8)

\displaystyle-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\Delta\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Let
\eta:=\sigma-\varepsilon\kappa_{1}>0
. Substituting (
4.2
), (
4.2
), and (
4.8
) into (
4.2
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\eta\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\sigma\kappa_{2}\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}||\boldsymbol{w}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\sigma\kappa_{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma\kappa_{1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\sigma\kappa_{2}\left\langle(\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\sigma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\varepsilon\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\varepsilon\kappa_{2}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{w},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\varepsilon\kappa_{2}\left\langle((\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{v},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\Delta\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{u}\times\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{u})-\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\langle\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{u})-\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{v}),\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.9)

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}+\cdots+I_{13}.

We will estimate each term on the right-hand side above. The first term is kept as is, while the second term is estimated using Young’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{2}}\right|\leq\frac{\sigma\kappa_{2}}{2}\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}||\boldsymbol{w}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma\kappa_{2}}{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the terms
I_{3}
and
I_{4}
, we apply (
2.19
) and (
2.20
) respectively to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{3}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\lambda_{1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\left|{I_{4}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the next two terms, by (
2.23
) and Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{5}}\right|+\left|{I_{6}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the terms
I_{7}
and
I_{8}
, by Young’s inequality we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{7}}\right|+\left|{I_{8}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\varepsilon\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the term
I_{9}
, similarly we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{9}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\varepsilon}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the term
I_{10}
, we use Young’s inequality and (
2.20
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{10}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma\kappa_{2}}{4}\left\|{|\boldsymbol{u}||\boldsymbol{w}|}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the term
I_{11}
, we apply (
2.48
) and Young’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{11}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{4}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Finally, for the last two terms, applying (
2.31
) and (
2.44
) we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{12}}\right|+\left|{I_{13}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{4}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Collecting all the above estimates and combining them with (
4.2
), we obtain

(4.10)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(1+\varepsilon^{-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

We note that by Agmon’s inequality, Proposition
3.1
, and Proposition
3.3
,

\int_{0}^{t}\left(1+\varepsilon^{-2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\mathrm{d}s\leq C(1+t)\left(1+\varepsilon^{-2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon^{-2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}+\varepsilon^{-2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\right).

Therefore, by the Gronwall inequality, we have the required inequality.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}(t)
and
\boldsymbol{v}(t)
be solutions of (
1.1
) corresponding to initial data
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}
and
\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}
, respectively. Then for any
t>0
,

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}(t)-\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{u}(s)-\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}(s)-\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq Ce^{Ct^{3}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}-\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2},

where
C
depends only on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}
,
\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}
, and the coefficients of the equation (
1.1
).

Proof.

Taking the inner product of (
4.2
) with
-\Delta\boldsymbol{w}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\sigma\left\langle\boldsymbol{B},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\sigma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\boldsymbol{B},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{1},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\boldsymbol{B},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.11)

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\left\langle\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{u})-\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\left\langle\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{u})-\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Taking the inner product of (
4.4
) with
-\sigma\Delta\boldsymbol{w}
, we have

\displaystyle-\sigma\left\langle\boldsymbol{B},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\sigma\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{1}\sigma\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{2}\sigma\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{w},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\sigma\left\langle((\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{v},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.12)

\displaystyle\quad-\sigma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Furthermore, applying the operator
\Delta
to (
4.4
) then taking the inner product of the result with
\varepsilon\Delta\boldsymbol{w}
, we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\boldsymbol{B},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{1}\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}-\kappa_{2}\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\big(|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\big),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.13)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa_{2}\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\big(((\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{v}\big),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Similarly, using (
4.4
), we have

\displaystyle\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\boldsymbol{B},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{1}\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\boldsymbol{w},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\kappa_{2}\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{w},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.14)

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\big(\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v})\big),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Writing
\eta=\sigma-\kappa_{1}\varepsilon
, we add (
4.2
), (
4.2
), (
4.2
), and (
4.2
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\eta\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{1}\sigma\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\kappa_{2}\sigma\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{w},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\sigma\left\langle((\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{v},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\sigma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\sigma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\kappa_{2}\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\big(|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\big),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\kappa_{2}\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\big(((\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{v})\cdot\boldsymbol{w})\boldsymbol{v}\big),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{1},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa_{1}\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\boldsymbol{w},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\kappa_{2}\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{w},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\big(\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v})\big),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\left\langle\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{u})-\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\left\langle\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{u})-\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.15)

\displaystyle=I_{1}+I_{2}+\cdots+I_{17}.

We will estimate each of the seventeen terms above in the following. The first term is kept as is. For the terms
I_{2}
and
I_{3}
, we apply Young’s inequality and the Sobolev embedding to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{2}\sigma\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{I_{3}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{2}\sigma\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}+\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the next term, using (
2.20
), Young’s inequality, and Sobolev embedding, we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{4}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\left\|{\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the terms
I_{6}
and
I_{7}
, integrating by parts, then applying (
2.12
) and Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{6}}\right|\leq\kappa_{2}\varepsilon\left\|{\nabla\big(|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\big)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq 2\kappa_{2}^{2}\varepsilon\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\left|{I_{7}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4\kappa_{2}^{2}\varepsilon\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+4\kappa_{2}^{2}\varepsilon\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the term
I_{8}
, we integrate by parts, then apply Young’s inequality and (
3
) with
p=q=6
to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{8}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\left\|{\nabla\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\nabla\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\varepsilon\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{8}\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where in the last step we also used the Sobolev embedding
\mathbb{H}^{2}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{W}^{1,6}
. For the terms
I_{5}
and
I_{9}
, by (
2.47
) and Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle\left|{I_{5}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\left\|{\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{I_{9}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\varepsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the term
I_{10}
, by Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, Sobolev embedding, and the definition of
\boldsymbol{H}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{10}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{H}_{1}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Similarly, for the next two terms, we have

\displaystyle\left|{I_{11}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{1}\gamma\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{I_{12}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{2}\gamma\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{6}}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the next term, we used Young’s inequality and (
2.20
) with
p=q=6
to infer

\displaystyle\left|{I_{13}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left\|{\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

For the terms
I_{14}
and
I_{15}
, we have by Young’s inequality and (
2.48
),

\displaystyle\left|{I_{14}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{I_{15}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w})}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Finally, for the last two terms we apply (
2.32
) and (
2.44
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left|{I_{16}}\right|+\left|{I_{17}}\right|\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{16}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Altogether, substituting these estimates into (
4.2
), we infer

(4.16)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon\left\|{\nabla\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+C\mathcal{B}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+C\varepsilon\left\|{\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})

\displaystyle:=1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{3}}^{2}+\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{4}\right)\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right).

We note that by Agmon’s inequality, Proposition
3.10
, and Theorem
3.11
,

\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{B}(\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{v})\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(1+t^{3})\left(1+\varepsilon^{-1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{6}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{6}\right).

Therefore, applying the Gronwall inequality on (
4.16
) and noting Lemma
4.7
, we have the required result.
∎

The following lemma shows a smoothing estimate for the difference of two solutions originating from different initial data.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
\boldsymbol{u}(t)
and
\boldsymbol{v}(t)
be solutions of (
1.1
) corresponding to initial data
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}
and
\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}
, respectively. Then for any
t>0
,

\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}(t)-\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\leq C(1+t^{-1})e^{Ct^{3}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}-\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2},

where
C
depends only on
\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}
,
\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}_{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}
, and the coefficients of the equation (
1.1
).

Proof.

Taking the inner product of (
4.2
) with
t\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{w}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(t\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{w}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+t\sigma\left\langle\Delta\boldsymbol{B},\Delta\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+t\sigma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-t\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\boldsymbol{B},\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-t\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-t\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}\times\boldsymbol{H}_{1},\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-t\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\boldsymbol{B},\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-t\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{w}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}),\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-t\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{w}),\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(4.17)

\displaystyle\quad+t\left\langle\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{u})-\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+t\left\langle\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{u})-\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}.

Applying the operator
\Delta
to (
4.4
), then taking the inner product of the result with
