\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{8}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C(1+\rho_{2})\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{8}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

where we also used (
2.31
) and (
2.44
) to estimate
I_{12}
and
I_{13}
respectively. Moreover, we used the inequalities (
5.13
), (
5.20
), and the Sobolev embedding in the second step for each inequality (if there is any). Altogether, substituting these into (
5.2
) and applying the Gronwall inequality, we obtain

(5.27)

\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}(s)}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C\varepsilon^{2}e^{Ct}.

Next, we take the inner product of (
5.2
) with
-\Delta\boldsymbol{v}
and integrate by parts as necessary to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\eta\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\sigma\kappa_{1}\left\|{\nabla\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\sigma\kappa_{2}\left\langle|\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\boldsymbol{v},\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\sigma\kappa_{2}\left\langle\big((\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}+\boldsymbol{u}^{0})\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\big)\boldsymbol{u}^{0},\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\sigma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{0}),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\sigma\left\langle\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon},\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\varepsilon\kappa_{2}\left\langle\Delta\big(|\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}\big),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\varepsilon\left\langle\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon},\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}^{0}\times\left(\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon})-\Phi_{\mathrm{a}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{0})\right),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}+\gamma\left\langle\boldsymbol{u}^{0}\times\Phi_{\mathrm{d}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\left\langle\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{R}(\boldsymbol{u}^{0}),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}-\left\langle\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon})-\mathcal{S}(\boldsymbol{u}^{0}),\Delta\boldsymbol{v}\right\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}

(5.28)

\displaystyle=:J_{1}+J_{2}+\cdots+J_{17}.

We estimate each term
J_{k}
, where
k=3,4,\ldots,17
, in the usual manner as follows:

\displaystyle\left|{J_{3}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\rho_{2}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{4}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{4}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\rho_{2}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{5}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{6}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{7}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\left\|{\Delta^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\rho_{4}\varepsilon^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{8}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{6}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\rho_{2}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{9}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\rho_{2}\varepsilon^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{10}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\rho_{2}\varepsilon^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{11}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\rho_{3}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{12}}\right|+\left|{J_{14}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\rho_{1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{13}}\right|+\left|{J_{15}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{4}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\rho_{1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{16}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(1+\rho_{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|{J_{17}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{\varepsilon}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}+\left\|{\boldsymbol{u}^{0}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}}^{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}\leq C\left(1+\rho_{2}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{18}\left\|{\Delta\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}^{2}.

Substituting these into (
5.2
), integrating over
(0,t)
, and using (
5.27
), we obtain the required result.
∎

We can finally state the main theorem of this section on the existence of a family of exponential attractors
\left\{\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}:\varepsilon\in[0,\sigma/\kappa_{1}]\right\}
for the semigroup
\boldsymbol{S}_{\varepsilon}(t)
generated by (
1.1
).

Theorem 5.10
.

Let
d\leq 2
, and let
\boldsymbol{S}_{\varepsilon}(t)
be the semigroup generated by (
1.1
) with
\lambda_{2}=0
. There exists a robust family of exponential attractors
\left\{\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}:\varepsilon\in[0,\sigma/\kappa_{1}]\right\}
for
\boldsymbol{S}_{\varepsilon}(t)
such that

(1)

the fractal dimension of
\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}
is bounded, uniformly with respect to
\varepsilon
,

(2)

for every bounded subsets
D\subset\mathbb{H}^{1}
, there exists a constant
c
depending on
D
such that

\text{dist}\left(\boldsymbol{S}_{\varepsilon}(t)D,\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}\right)\leq ce^{-\alpha t},\quad\forall t\geq 0,

where the positive constant
c
and
\alpha
are independent of
\varepsilon
,

(3)

the family
\left\{\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon}:\varepsilon\in[0,\sigma/\kappa_{1}]\right\}
is Hölder continuous at
0
, namely

\text{dist}_{\text{sym}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{\varepsilon},\mathcal{M}_{0}\right)\leq c\varepsilon^{k},

where
c\geq 0
,
k\in(0,1)
are independent of
\varepsilon
, and
\text{dist}_{\text{sym}}
is the symmetric Hausdorff distance defined in (
A.5
).

Proof.

We apply Theorem
A.4
with
X=\mathbb{H}^{2}
,
H=\mathbb{H}^{1}
, and
B=\mathbb{B}
. The hypotheses of Theorem
A.4
are verified in Lemma
5.8
and Lemma
5.9
. Lipschitz continuity on
\mathbb{H}^{1}
and Hölder continuity in time have been verified before in Theorem
4.16
. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Appendix A
Auxiliary results

In this section, we collect some inequalities and results which are extensively used in this paper.

Theorem A.1

(The uniform Gronwall inequality)
.

Let
g
,
h
, and
y
be non-negative locally integrable functions on
(t_{0},\infty)
such that
\mathrm{d}y/\mathrm{d}t
is locally integrable on
(t_{0},\infty)
, and which satisfy

(A.1)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq g(t)y(t)+h(t),\quad\forall t\geq t_{0}.

Let
r>0
. Suppose that there exist
a_{1}
,
a_{2}
,
a_{3}\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{t+r}g(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq a_{1},\quad\int_{t}^{t+r}h(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq a_{2},\quad\int_{t}^{t+r}y(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq a_{3},\quad\forall t\geq t_{0}.

Then

\displaystyle y(t+r)\leq\left(\frac{a_{3}}{r}+a_{2}\right)\exp(a_{1}),\quad\forall t\geq t_{0}.

Corollary A.2
.

Suppose that the assumptions of Theorem
A.1
hold with
r=1
. Furthermore, suppose that for all
t>t_{0}
,

(A.2)

\int_{t_{0}}^{t}g(s)y(s)+h(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq P(t),

where
P(t)
is a non-negative function of
t
. Then for all
t>t_{0}
,

y(t)\leq\left(\frac{a_{3}}{t-t_{0}}+2a_{2}+a_{3}\right)\exp(a_{1})+P(t).

Proof.

By the uniform Gronwall inequality, the assumptions imply that for any
\delta\in(0,1]
,

y(t_{0}+\delta)\leq\left(\frac{a_{3}}{\delta}+a_{2}\right)\exp(a_{1}),

or equivalently, for any
t\in(t_{0},t_{0}+1]
,

(A.3)

y(t)\leq\left(\frac{a_{3}}{t-t_{0}}+a_{2}\right)\exp(a_{1})

Integrating (
A.1
) over
(t_{0}+1,t)
, using (
A.2
) and (
A.3
), we obtain for all
t\geq t_{0}+1
,

(A.4)

y(t)\leq y(t_{0}+1)+\int_{t_{0}+1}^{t}g(s)y(s)+h(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\left(a_{3}+a_{2}\right)\exp(a_{1})+P(t).

Combining (
A.3
) and (
A.4
) yields the required inequality.
∎

Theorem A.3

(Theorem 2, Section 3.3.3 in
[
19
]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{O}
be a regular bounded domain. Let
M:=\mathbb{W}^{s_{1},p_{1}}(\mathscr{O})
and let
X
be the unit ball of the space
\mathbb{W}^{s_{2},p_{2}}(\mathscr{O})
with

\frac{1}{p_{1}}-\frac{s_{1}}{d}>\frac{1}{p_{2}}-\frac{s_{2}}{d}.

Then
X
is pre-compact in
M
, and thus the Kolmogorov
\epsilon
-entropy of
X
(considered as a compact subset of
M
), denoted by
\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}(X)
, is well-defined and satisfies

C_{1}\epsilon^{-d/(s_{2}-s_{1})}\leq\mathcal{H}_{\epsilon}(X)\leq C_{2}\epsilon^{-d/(s_{2}-s_{1})},

where
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are independent of
\epsilon
.

Theorem A.4

(
[
22
,
40
]
)
.

Let
X
and
H
be two Hilbert spaces such that
X\hookrightarrow H
is a compact embedding. Suppose that for every
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
,
S_{\epsilon}(t):B\to B
is a strongly continuous semigroup acting on a semi-invariant absorbing set
B\subset X
. Suppose further that

(1)

there exists a non-negative function
\kappa(t)
, independent of
\epsilon
, such that for every
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
and every
u,v\in B
,

\left\|{S_{\epsilon}(t)u-S_{\epsilon}(t)v}\right\|_{X}\leq\kappa(t)\left\|{u-v}\right\|_{H},

(2)

there exists a constant
c
, independent of
\epsilon
, such that for every
t\geq 0
and every
u\in B
,

\left\|{S_{\epsilon}(t)u-S_{0}(t)u}\right\|_{H}\leq c\epsilon e^{ct},

(3)

for every
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
,
T>0
, and
u\in B
, the map
t\mapsto S_{\epsilon}(t)u
is Hölder continuous on
[0,T]
;

(4)

for every
\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
and
t\geq 0
, the map
u\mapsto S_{\epsilon}(t)u
is Lipschitz continuous on
B
.

Then there exists a family of robust exponential attractors
\{\mathcal{M}_{\epsilon}:\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]\}
on
H
such that

(1)

the fractal dimension of
\mathcal{M}_{\epsilon}
is bounded, uniformly with respect to
\epsilon
,

(2)

\mathcal{M}_{\epsilon}
attracts bounded subsets
D\subset H
exponentially fast, uniformly with respect to
\epsilon
, namely there exists a constant
c
depending on
D
such that

\text{dist}\left(S_{\epsilon}(t)D,\mathcal{M}_{\epsilon}\right)\leq ce^{-\alpha t},\quad\forall t\geq 0,

where the positive constant
c
and
\alpha
are independent of
\epsilon
,

(3)

the family
\{\mathcal{M}_{\epsilon}:\epsilon\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]\}
is Hölder continuous at
0
, namely

\text{dist}_{\text{sym}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{M}_{0}\right)\leq c\epsilon^{k},

where
c\geq 0
and
k\in(0,1)
are independent of
\epsilon
. Here,
\text{dist}_{\text{sym}}
denotes the symmetric Hausdorff distance between sets defined by

(A.5)

\text{dist}_{\text{sym}}(A,B):=\max\big(\text{dist}(A,B),\text{dist}(B,A)\big).
